330520 Let $S^n$ be the unit sphere with its rotation-invariant volume, and let $A\subseteq S^n$ be measurable. Suppose that $A$ contains no two orthogonal vectors. **Witsenhausen's conjecture.** The volume of $A$ is at most twice the volume of two spherical caps of radius $\pi/4$. Witsenhausen proved the weaker bound $\mu(A)\leq 1/(n+1)$; the supplied text does not specify whether the conjecture has been resolved.
330525 A tangent graph has one vertex for each disc and an edge when the corresponding discs are tangent. **Pinchasi–Sharir tangent-incidence conjecture.** (i) Planar tangent graphs with $n$ vertices have at most $n^{4/3}\operatorname{polylog}(n)$ edges. (ii) More generally, $m$ red discs and $n$ blue discs, including the special case of $n$ blue points, can touch at most $((mn)^{2/3}+m+n)\operatorname{polylog}(m,n)$ times. The source reports a best known upper bound of $n^{3/2}\log n$ and leaves the conjecture open.
330604 Let $M$ be a matroid with set of bases $\mathcal{B}$, and let

$$
P(M):=\operatorname{conv}\{e_B:B\in\mathcal{B}\},
$$

where $e_B$ is the sum of the standard basis vectors indexed by $B$. A triangulation is **regular unimodular** when it is induced by a lifting function and every simplex is unimodular. **Haws' conjecture.** For any matroid $M$, the polytope $P(M)$ has a regular unimodular triangulation. The source describes this as a geometric variant of White's algebraic conjecture and gives no resolution.
330649 An LP algebra is an algebra defined from seeds and mutations, and a **cluster monomial** is a product of powers of variables $X_1^{a_1}\dots X_n^{a_n}$ whose variables $X_1,\dots,X_n$ belong to the same seed, with $a_1,\dots,a_n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\ge 0}$. **The cluster monomial basis conjecture.** For any finite-type LP algebra, the set of cluster monomials forms a linear basis of the subring $\mathcal{A}$. This conjecture concerns the linear and combinatorial structure of finite-type LP algebras; the source presents it as a conjecture posed in earlier work and suggests that recursive exchange polynomials may make it easier to verify for product and sum algebras.
330763 Let $\mathcal{L}$ be the lattice of biased dessins, let $\Gamma' = \operatorname{Aut}(\mathcal{L})$, and let $\Gamma'_0$ be the subgroup of $\Gamma'$ that fixes the three vertices of the bottom element $D_1$. The absolute Galois group $\Gamma$ is a subgroup of $\Gamma'_0$. **Equality conjecture.**

$$
\Gamma = \Gamma'_0.
$$

The preceding theorem establishes the inclusion, while the source states that there is currently no evidence or proof strategy for the reverse inclusion.
330783 Let $K$ be an algebraically closed field of characteristic $0$, let $w(x_1,\dots,x_m)$ be a nontrivial group word, and let $\operatorname{PSL}_2(K)$ denote the projective linear group. **Word-image conjecture.** The image of $w$ on $\operatorname{PSL}_2(K)$ is

$$
\operatorname{PSL}_2(K).
$$

This conjecture asks whether every nontrivial word map is surjective on $\operatorname{PSL}_2(K)$. The image is known to contain all non-unipotent elements, while the question of whether the nontrivial unipotent class lies in the image remains open.
330784 Let $K$ be an algebraically closed field of characteristic $0$, let $w(x_1,\dots,x_m)$ be a group word whose degree in each variable $x_i$ is $0$, and set

$$
G=\operatorname{GL}_2(K)/\{\pm 1\}.
$$

**Degree-zero word reformulation.** The image of $w$ on $G$ must be

$$
\operatorname{PSL}_2(K).
$$

This is presented in the source as a reformulation of the word-image conjecture after reducing to words of degree zero in every variable. It remains open in the stated generality.
330793 Consider a three-pile Sharing Nim position $(0,a,b)$ with $a\leq b/2$, and let $\mathcal{G}(0,a,b)$ be its nim-value.

**Support-bound conjecture.** If $\mathcal{G}(0,a,b)=g\geq 2$, then

$$
a\leq 2g-1.
$$

This is an empirical estimate based on computations: nim-values $g\geq 2$ appear to be distributed among the bottom $2g$ rows or fewer. The source gives no proof or resolution and notes that the bound may be an overestimate.
330850 Let $a\geq 3$ be an integer, and consider the sequence

$$
\left(\left\lfloor \frac{n^2}{a}\right\rfloor\right)_{n\in\mathbb{N}}.
$$

**Farhi's conjecture.** Every natural number can be represented as the sum of three terms of this sequence.

This conjecture concerns additive representations by a quadratic-floor sequence and is motivated by classical three-square representation results. The paper proves some cases of the conjecture, while the statement in full remains open.
330884 Let $G$ be a subcubic graph, meaning that every vertex of $G$ has degree at most $3$. Let $\chi_{\nu}(G)$ denote the new vertex chromatic number introduced in the paper. **Subcubic vertex-chromatic-index conjecture.**

$$
\chi_{\nu}(G)\le 4.
$$

The conjecture is part of the paper's program of using the new vertex chromatic number to establish acyclic edge-colouring results for Cartesian products. The supplied text does not state whether it has been resolved.
330892 Let $\hat{G}$ be a block and hole graph with one pentagonal block and two quadrilateral holes, or, two quadrilateral blocks and one pentagonal hole. **The 5-connectivity conjecture.** If $\hat{G}$ is $5$-connected, then it is minimally $3$-rigid. This is one of the conjectures paraphrased from earlier work; the surrounding theorem establishes the corresponding single-block and single-hole cases, while the general situation addressed here is not resolved by the supplied text.
330909 Let $G$ be a graph. A subgraph is **isometric** if distances between its vertices agree with their distances in $G$, and $G$ is **distance preserving** if it has an isometric subgraph of every order from $1$ to $|V(G)|$.

**Induced-cycle conjecture.** If $G$ does not contain an induced cycle of length $5$ or greater, then $G$ is a distance-preserving graph.

This is presented among the paper's questions and conjectures about distance-preserving graphs. The supplied text gives no resolution, so the conjecture remains open.
330970 A **COM** is a complex of oriented matroids, and an **OM** is an oriented matroid. A **fiber** of a COM is an intersection of its halfspaces. The conjecture is that every COM can be obtained in this way from an OM.

**Fiber representation conjecture.** Every COM is a fiber of some OM.

This would generalize the realizable situation and, together with the Topological Representation Theorem for oriented matroids, give a natural topological representation theorem for COMs. The source presents the claim as open.
330972 Suppose the edges of an infinite complete bipartite graph are coloured with $rin \omega$ colours. Then its vertices can be partitioned into $2r-1$ disjoint monochromatic paths.

**Infinite bipartite monochromatic path conjecture.** The vertices can be partitioned into $2r-1$ disjoint monochromatic paths.

This asks whether the finite-colour path-decomposition result extends from infinite complete graphs to infinite complete bipartite graphs; the statement is presented as an open problem.
331004 Let $G$ be a connected graph of order $n+s$ and size $m+s$, in which $s$ vertices have degree $1$ and all other vertices have degree $k$. Let $S(G)$ be the graph obtained by inserting a new vertex into each edge of $G$, let $L(S(G))$ be its line graph, and let $t(H)$ denote the number of spanning trees of a graph $H$. **Yan's conjecture.**

$$
t(L(S(G)))=k^{m+s-n-1}(k+2)^{m-n+1}t(G).
$$

This conjecture extends the known spanning-tree formula for regular graphs to connected graphs with $s$ degree-one vertices and all remaining vertices of degree $k$. The source presents it as a proposed generalization; its status is not established in the supplied text.
331080 Let $n\in\mathbb{N}$ and let $a,b,c$ be integers satisfying $1<a,b,c\leq n-2$. For permutations $x,y\in S_n$, say that they have prescribed orders $a,b,c$ when $x$ has order $a$, $y$ has order $b$, and $xy$ has order $c$. **Kohl's conjecture.** There exist elements $x,y\in S_n$ such that $x$ has order $a$, $y$ has order $b$, and $xy$ has order $c$. The conjecture concerns the existence of permutation triples with prescribed orders and product $1$, equivalently by taking $z=(xy)^{-1}$. The paper's abstract states that this conjecture is proved, so it is solved.
331251 Let $G$ be a $3$-connected $3$-indivisible infinite planar graph. A **$2$-way infinite $2$-walk** is a doubly infinite spanning walk in which every vertex is visited at most twice.

**The 2-way infinite 2-walk conjecture.** The graph $G$ has a $2$-way infinite $2$-walk.

The conjecture is the natural two-ended analogue of the paper's proved one-way result and extends earlier locally finite results by Timar. The text provides no resolution, so the conjecture remains open.
331259 Let $K$ be a web in $\R^{3}$, meaning an embedded trivalent graph, and let $\Jsharp(K)$ be the associated finite-dimensional vector space over the field $\F$ of two elements. A **Tait coloring** of $K$ is an assignment of the colors $\{1,2,3\}$ to its edges such that the three edges incident at every vertex have distinct colors. If $K$ lies in the plane, so that $K\subset\R^{2}\subset\R^{3}$, then **the planar web dimension conjecture** says that

$$
\dim_{\F}\Jsharp(K)=\#\{\text{Tait colorings of }K\}.
$$

The conjecture predicts that, for planar webs, the instanton-homological invariant is determined by the combinatorics of Tait colorings. The paper presents it as plausible evidence-based speculation; no resolution is supplied here.
331376 Let $(\phi_n)$ be a sequence of Laplace eigenfunctions, and let $\mu(\phi_n)$ denote the number of nodal domains of $\phi_n$. Let $Sq_1$ be the square of area $1$, with first eigenvalue $\lambda(Sq_1)$. **Polterovich's conjecture.**

$$
\limsup_{n\rightarrow +\infty}\frac{\mu(\phi_n)}{n}\leq \frac{4\pi}{\lambda(Sq_1)}=\frac{2}{\pi}.
$$

The source presents this as a consequence that would follow if suitable bipartite minimal partitions satisfied the stated square-tiling lower bound; it is not established here.
331377 Let $G$ be a vertex-transitive partial cube, and suppose that its girth is six.

**Middle-level graph conjecture.** The graph $G$ is a middle-level graph.

The conjecture proposes that the known middle-level graphs exhaust the vertex-transitive partial cubes of girth six. The classification in the paper establishes the claim for the cases covered by its results, while the broader classification problem for higher-valency vertex-transitive partial cubes remains open.

{\n      \"mathdb_id\": 330162,\n      \"uuid\": \"7dd13191-e584-4231-8972-95787f709df6\",\n      \"canonical_url\": \"https://mathdb.com/p/330162/j170-three-weight-code-conjecture\",\n CONTENTS.md # Mathematics manuscript collection

**719 manuscripts covering 372 result families.**

[**Read the overview PDF**](overview.pdf).

## Manuscript map

Each result description is followed by its constituent manuscripts and their abstracts. Paper titles link directly to PDFs.

<table>
<thead><tr><th>Result</th></tr></thead>
<tbody><tr>
<td>

**001. Milne’s rationality conjecture and algebraic specialization.** Proves Milne's rationality conjecture for abelian varieties over $`\overline{\mathbb Q}`$ with good reduction: specialized Hodge classes pair rationally with complementary divisor products, independently of cohomology theory. Together with result 032, every specialized Hodge class is represented by a single rational algebraic cycle simultaneously in all prime-to-<i>p</i> and crystalline realizations, for every residue characteristic <i>p</i>.

</td>
</tr></tbody>
<tbody><tr>
<td>

&emsp;[Milne's rationality conjecture for abelian varieties](preprints/Milnes-rationality-conjecture-for-abelian-varieties-October-7-2026/paper.pdf)

We prove Milne's rationality conjecture for abelian varieties, including residue characteristic 2. After good reduction, the pairing of a rational Hodge class with any complementary product of divisor classes on the reduction is the same rational number in every prime-to-<i>p</i> realization and in crystalline cohomology. Using the Hodge theorem for CM abelian varieties, we also show that every such specialized Hodge class is represented by a single rational algebraic cycle in all these realizations.

</td>
</tr></tbody>
<tbody><tr>
<td>

**002. The full BSD formula from low Selmer corank.** Proves the full Birch–Swinnerton-Dyer leading-term formula for every elliptic curve over ℚ whose full <i>q</i>-power Selmer group has corank zero or one for some prime <i>q</i>, including finiteness of the Tate–Shafarevich group. With result 006, this gives full BSD for a density-one set of quadratic twists of every elliptic curve over ℚ.

</td>
</tr></tbody>
<tbody><tr>
<td>

&emsp;[Exact Birch–Swinnerton-Dyer Formula from Low Selmer Corank](preprints/Exact-Birch-Swinnerton-Dyer-Formula-from-Low-Selmer-Corank-October-7-2026/exact-bsd-low-selmer-corank.pdf)

We prove the full Birch–Swinnerton-Dyer leading-term formula for every elliptic curve over ℚ whose full <i>q</i>-power Selmer group has corank zero or one at some prime <i>q</i>. The analytic and Mordell–Weil ranks equal that corank, and the Tate–Shafarevich group is finite. The formula includes all prime factors and requires no additional hypotheses on reduction, rational torsion, isogenies, complex multiplication, or residual Galois representations.

</td>
</tr></tbody>
<tbody><tr>
<td>

&emsp;[The Selmer converse for elliptic curves at every prime](preprints/The-Selmer-converse-for-elliptic-curves-at-every-prime-October-7-2026/main.pdf)

We prove the Selmer converse in coranks zero and one for every elliptic curve over ℚ and every prime <i>p</i>: if the full <i>p</i>-power Selmer group has ℤ<sub><i>p</i></sub>-corank $`r\in\{0,1\}`$, then the analytic and Mordell–Weil ranks both equal <i>r</i>, and the entire Tate–Shafarevich group is finite. As an application at the additive prime 3, we prove that for every prime $`\ell\equiv4,7,8\pmod9`$, the cubic $`X^3+Y^3=\ell Z^3`$ has analytic and Mordell–Weil rank one and finite Tate–Shafarevich group. In particular, every such <i>ℓ</i> is a sum of two rational cubes.

</td>
</tr></tbody>
<tbody><tr>
<td>

&emsp;[The two-primary Birch–Swinnerton-Dyer formula in Selmer corank at most one](preprints/The-two-primary-Birch-Swinnerton-Dyer-formula-in-Selmer-corank-at-most-one-October-6-2026/paper.pdf)

We prove the two-primary Birch and Swinnerton-Dyer leading-term formula for every elliptic curve over the rationals whose two-power Selmer group has corank at most one. In this range, the algebraic rank, analytic rank, and Selmer corank are equal, and the Tate–Shafarevich group is finite. Combined with the quadratic-twist Selmer distribution, this gives the exact two-primary formula for a density-one set of signed squarefree twists of each fixed curve, ordered by absolute value; the common rank is zero or one, with each value having density one half.

</td>
</tr></tbody>
<tbody><tr>
<td>

**003. The quasi-Riemann hypothesis.** Proves that every Dirichlet <i>L</i>-function, including $`\zeta(s)`$, is zero-free in $`\Re s\gt 7/8`$, resolving the quasi-Riemann hypothesis. The same half-plane is zero-free for every finite-order Hecke <i>L</i>-function over $`\mathbb Q(\sqrt{-3})`$. A companion gives a different proof of the zero-free half-plane $`\Re s\gt 11/12`$. ([Lean](lean/docs/003.md))

</td>
</tr></tbody>
<tbody><tr>
<td>

&emsp;[The Quasi-Riemann Hypothesis: A Zero-Free Half-Plane $`\Re s\gt 7/8`$ ](preprints/The-Quasi-Riemann-Hypothesis-September-30-2026/paper.pdf)

We prove that all finite-order Hecke <i>L</i>-functions over $`\mathbb Q(\sqrt{-3})`$ and all Dirichlet <i>L</i>-functions are zero-free in the half-plane $`\Re s\gt 7/8`$, with the principal pole at <i>s</i> = 1 allowed. In particular, the Riemann zeta function is zero-free in this half-plane, proving the quasi-Riemann hypothesis.

</td>
</tr></tbody>
<tbody><tr>
<td>

&emsp;[The Quasi-Riemann Hypothesis (alternate 11/12 proof)](preprints/The-Quasi-Riemann-Hypothesis-October-5-2026/paper2.pdf)

We establish the quasi-Riemann hypothesis by proving that every Dirichlet <i>L</i>-function, including Riemann's zeta function, has no zeros in the half-plane $`\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}\nolimits s\gt 11/12`$. More generally, we prove the same zero-free half-plane for every finite-order Hecke <i>L</i>-function over $`K=\mathbb Q(\sqrt{-3})`$. In particular, this rules out the existence of Landau–Siegel zeros.

</td>
</tr></tbody>
<tbody><tr>
<td>

&emsp;[Uniform exclusion of Landau–Siegel zeros](preprints/Uniform-exclusion-of-Landau-Siegel-zeros-October-1-2026/paper.pdf)

We prove the uniform exclusion of Landau–Siegel zeros. There is an absolute constant <i>c</i> &gt; 0 such that every real zero $`\beta\in(0,1)`$ of every primitive nonprincipal real Dirichlet <i>L</i>-function of conductor <i>q</i> ≥ 3 satisfies $`(1-\beta)\log q\ge c`$.

</td>
</tr></tbody>
<tbody><tr>
<td>

**004. Hilbert’s tenth problem over ℚ.** Proves that no algorithm decides whether an integer-coefficient polynomial in an arbitrary number of variables has a rational zero, resolving Hilbert's tenth problem over ℚ negatively.

</td>
</tr></tbody>
<tbody><tr>
<td>

&emsp;[Hilbert’s tenth problem over the rational numbers](preprints/Hilberts-tenth-problem-over-the-rational-numbers-October-6-2026/main.pdf)

We give a negative answer to Hilbert's tenth problem over the rational numbers: no algorithm decides whether a polynomial with integer coefficients has a rational zero. The number of variables is part of the input.

</td>
</tr></tbody>
<tbody><tr>
<td>

&emsp;[A pointwise 2-converse for elliptic curves with rational two-torsion](preprints/A-pointwise-2-converse-for-elliptic-curves-with-rational-two-torsion-October-7-2026/paper.pdf)

We prove a pointwise 2-converse for elliptic curves over $`\mathbf Q`$ with nonzero rational two-torsion: if the $`2^\infty`$-Selmer corank is zero or one, then the analytic rank and Mordell–Weil rank equal that corank, and the Shafarevich–Tate group is finite. The result allows arbitrary reduction at 2.

</td>
</tr></tbody>
<tbody><tr>
<td>

**005. Irrationality of Catalan’s constant.** Proves that Catalan's constant $`G=\sum_{j\ge0}(-1)^j/(2j+1)^2`$ is irrational. ([Lean](lean/docs/005.md))

</td>
</tr></tbody>
<tbody><tr>
<td>

&emsp;[Catalan's constant is irrational](preprints/Catalans-constant-is-irrational-September-24-2026/paper.pdf)

We prove that Catalan's constant $`G=\sum_{j\geq0}(-1)^j/(2j+1)^2`$ is irrational.

</td>
</tr></tbody>
<tbody><tr>
<td>

**006. Goldfeld’s conjecture: densities and mean analytic rank.** Proves Goldfeld's conjecture for quadratic twists of every elliptic curve over ℚ: analytic ranks zero and one each have density 1/2, and the mean analytic rank tends to 1/2. Both statements order signed squarefree twist parameters by absolute value.

</td>
</tr></tbody>
<tbody><tr>
<td>

&emsp;[Goldfeld's analytic density conjecture and the 2-converse for elliptic curves](preprints/Goldfelds-analytic-density-conjecture-and-the-2-converse-for-elliptic-curves-October-7-2026/paper.pdf)

We prove Goldfeld's analytic density conjecture: for every elliptic curve <i>E</i> over ℚ, the quadratic twists of <i>E</i> with analytic rank zero and one each have density 1/2 among signed squarefree twist parameters ordered by absolute value. We also prove the low-corank 2-converse: if the $`2^\infty`$-Selmer corank of <i>E</i> is zero or one, then it equals the analytic and Mordell–Weil ranks, and the Tate–Shafarevich group is finite.

</td>
</tr></tbody>
<tbody><tr>
<td>

&emsp;[The mean analytic rank of quadratic twists of elliptic curves](preprints/The-mean-analytic-rank-of-quadratic-twists-of-elliptic-curves-October-6-2026/paper.pdf)

For every elliptic curve over ℚ, we prove that the average analytic rank of its quadratic twists tends to 1/2 when signed squarefree twist parameters are ordered by absolute value. This resolves Goldfeld's mean analytic-rank conjecture in this counting convention.

</td>
</tr></tbody>
<tbody><tr>
<td>

**007. Ordinary two-point correlations and the corrected Elliott conjecture.** Proves the ordinary two-point Chowla conjecture, with a bound $`O(X/(\log X)^c)`$ for Liouville correlation sums along fixed nonproportional affine forms, where <i>c</i> &gt; 0 is absolute. More generally, proves the binary corrected Elliott conjecture for complex multiplicative functions bounded by one when one factor is uniformly nonpretentious against each fixed Dirichlet character times $`n^{it}`$ for $`|t|\le X`$. ([Lean](lean/docs/007.md))

</td>
</tr></tbody>
<tbody><tr>
<td>

&emsp;[Ordinary two-point correlations of multiplicative functions](preprints/Ordinary-two-point-correlations-of-multiplicative-functions-September-24-2026/final.pdf)

We prove the ordinary two-point Chowla conjecture. For every fixed pair of nonproportional affine forms, the Liouville correlation has a power-of-logarithm saving at every cutoff, with an absolute exponent. We also prove the binary corrected Elliott conjecture for ordinary averages of complex multiplicative functions of modulus at most one, under uniform nonpretentiousness of at least one original factor. This qualitative conclusion holds in fixed residue classes and for fixed nonproportional affine forms.

</td>
</tr></tbody>
<tbody><tr>
<td>

**008. The Deligne–Drinfeld conjecture.** Proves that the rational Grothendieck–Teichmüller Lie algebra, with the Ihara bracket, is freely generated by one element in each odd weight 3, 5, 7, …, resolving the Deligne–Drinfeld conjecture. ([Lean](lean/docs/008.md))

</td>
</tr></tbody>
<tbody><tr>
<td>

&emsp;[The Deligne-Drinfeld conjecture](preprints/The-Deligne-Drinfeld-conjecture-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)

We prove the Deligne–Drinfeld conjecture: the rational Grothendieck–Teichmüller Lie algebra is freely generated by one element in every odd weight at least three, with the corresponding isomorphism after weight completion.

</td>
</tr></tbody>
<tbody><tr>
<td>

**009. Function-field reconstruction from Milnor K-theory and Galois data.** Reconstructs function fields of transcendence degree at least two over algebraically closed constants from $`K^{\mathrm M}_1/\ell`$, $`K^{\mathrm M}_2/\ell`$, and their product. These data recover the perfect closure and constants when <i>ℓ</i> differs from the characteristic, and the original field and its named base in equal characteristic. Also proves Bogomolov–Pop reconstruction from abelian-by-central pro-<i>ℓ</i> Galois data away from the characteristic. ([Lean](lean/docs/009.md))

</td>
</tr></tbody>
<tbody><tr>
<td>

&emsp;[Reconstruction of Function Fields from M
